New Error: `Execute-Step: ... Error executing step GENERATE_TASK_TYPE_QUESTION: The term 'Execute-StepAction' is not recognized as a name of a cmdlet, function, script file, or executable program.`

This error occurs inside the `Execute-Step` function (which is in `_step_execution.ps1`) when it tries to call `Execute-StepAction`.
The `Execute-Step` function has a `switch ($stepType)`:
```powershell
# Inside Execute-Step in _step_execution.ps1
switch ($stepType) {
    "Question" {
        $stepResult = Execute-StepQuestion -StepDefinition $StepDefinition -Context $Context -UserInputValue $UserInputValue -EngineConfiguration $EngineConfiguration
    }
    "Action" {
        # THIS IS THE CASE FOR GENERATE_TASK_TYPE_QUESTION
        $stepResult = Execute-StepAction -StepDefinition $StepDefinition -Context $Context -EngineConfiguration $EngineConfiguration
    }
    "Decision" { # ... }
    "Info" { # ...}
    default { # ... }
}
```
The step `GENERATE_TASK_TYPE_QUESTION` has `type: "Action"`.
So `Execute-Step` tries to call `Execute-StepAction`.
The error means that `Execute-StepAction` is not found.

But `Execute-StepAction` *is* defined in `_step_execution.ps1` (the more complex version, after I removed the simpler duplicate). And `_step_execution.ps1` *is* being dot-sourced by `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1`.

Why would it not be found?
Possibilities:
1.  Typo in the function name in the definition or the call. (Checked, `Execute-StepAction` matches).
2.  The dot-sourcing of `_step_execution.ps1` failed silently, or the definition of `Execute-StepAction` within it has a syntax error that prevents it from being loaded, but doesn't stop the whole script.

When `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1` sources partials, it uses:
`Get-ChildItem -Path $partialsDir -Filter "*.ps1" | ForEach-Object { Write-Verbose "Sourcing partial: $($_.FullName)"; . $_.FullName }`
If `. $_.FullName` (for `_step_execution.ps1`) encounters a non-terminating parser error within `Execute-StepAction`'s definition, the function might not be correctly added to the scope, but the loop continues to source other partials. The main script then runs, only failing when `Execute-StepAction` is called.

The error message `The term 'Execute-StepAction' is not recognized...` usually indicates a complete absence from the current scope.

Let's add a `Write-Host` immediately after dot-sourcing `_step_execution.ps1` to check if `Execute-StepAction` is a known command *at that point*.
This means modifying the loop in `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1`:

```powershell
Get-ChildItem -Path $partialsDir -Filter "*.ps1" | ForEach-Object {
    Write-Verbose "Sourcing partial: $($_.FullName)"
    . $_.FullName
    if ($_.Name -eq "_step_execution.ps1") {
        if (Get-Command Execute-StepAction -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue) {
            Write-Host "[DEBUG] Execute-StepAction IS RECOGNIZED immediately after sourcing _step_execution.ps1" -ForegroundColor Green
        } else {
            Write-Warning "[DEBUG] Execute-StepAction IS NOT RECOGNIZED immediately after sourcing _step_execution.ps1"
        }
    }
}
```

The complex `Execute-StepAction` definition (from my memory of our previous debugging steps, and it was the one I kept after trying to delete the "other" one):
```powershell
function Execute-StepAction {
    param(
        [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][PSCustomObject]$StepDefinition,
        [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][hashtable]$Context,
        [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][hashtable]$EngineConfiguration
    )

    # --- BEGIN DEBUG ---    
    Write-Host "DEBUG [Execute-StepAction]: Received EngineConfiguration keys: $($EngineConfiguration.Keys | Out-String)" -ForegroundColor Yellow
    if ($EngineConfiguration.ContainsKey('EnginePSScriptRoot')) {
        Write-Host "DEBUG [Execute-StepAction]: EnginePSScriptRoot IS PRESENT. Value: $($EngineConfiguration.EnginePSScriptRoot)" -ForegroundColor Green
    } else {
        Write-Host "DEBUG [Execute-StepAction]: EnginePSScriptRoot IS MISSING or key name is wrong!" -ForegroundColor Red
    }
    # --- END DEBUG --- 

    $result = @{ success = $false; output = $null; nextStepId = $null; error = $null }

    if (-not $StepDefinition.action) {
        throw "Action definition missing for step $($StepDefinition.id)"
    }

    $actionType = $StepDefinition.action.type
    Write-Verbose "Executing action type: $actionType"

    try {
        switch ($actionType) {
            "powershell" {
                $scriptBlockText = $null
                $scriptPathFromScenario = $StepDefinition.action.scriptPath
                $externalScriptParameters = $StepDefinition.action.parameters

                if (-not [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($scriptPathFromScenario)) {
                    $projectRoot = $null
                    if ($EngineConfiguration.ContainsKey('EnginePSScriptRoot')) {
                        $enginePSScriptRoot = $EngineConfiguration.EnginePSScriptRoot
                        try {
                            $projectRoot = (Resolve-Path (Join-Path $enginePSScriptRoot "..", "..")).Path 
                        } catch {
                             throw "Could not determine project root from EnginePSScriptRoot ('$enginePSScriptRoot'). Error: $($_.Exception.Message)"
                        }
                    } else {
                        throw "EnginePSScriptRoot not found in EngineConfiguration. Cannot reliably determine project root to resolve scriptPath: '$scriptPathFromScenario'."
                    }

                    if (-not $projectRoot) { 
                        throw "Project root could not be determined. Cannot resolve scriptPath: '$scriptPathFromScenario'."
                    }

                    $normalizedPathFromScenario = $scriptPathFromScenario
                    if ($normalizedPathFromScenario.StartsWith("./")) {
                        $normalizedPathFromScenario = $normalizedPathFromScenario.Substring(2)
                    }
                    
                    $finalResolvedScriptPath = Join-Path $projectRoot $normalizedPathFromScenario
                    
                    Write-Verbose "Attempting to load PowerShell script. ProjectRoot: '$projectRoot', ScenarioScriptPath: '$scriptPathFromScenario', NormalizedPath: '$normalizedPathFromScenario', ResolvedPath: '$finalResolvedScriptPath'"

                    if (-not (Test-Path $finalResolvedScriptPath -PathType Leaf)) {
                        $fallbackPath = Join-Path $EngineConfiguration.EnginePSScriptRoot $scriptPathFromScenario 
                        Write-Verbose "Primary resolved path '$finalResolvedScriptPath' not found. Trying fallback relative to EnginePSScriptRoot: '$fallbackPath'"
                        if (Test-Path $fallbackPath -PathType Leaf) {
                            $finalResolvedScriptPath = $fallbackPath
                            Write-Verbose "Using fallback path: '$finalResolvedScriptPath'"
                        } else {
                            throw "PowerShell script file not found. Tried project-relative path '$finalResolvedScriptPath' and engine-relative path '$fallbackPath'."
                        }
                    }
                    
                    Write-Verbose "Loading PowerShell script from path: $finalResolvedScriptPath"
                    $scriptBlockText = Get-Content -Path $finalResolvedScriptPath -Raw -ErrorAction Stop
                } elseif (-not [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($StepDefinition.action.script)) {
                    Write-Verbose "Using inline PowerShell script."
                    $scriptBlockText = $StepDefinition.action.script
                } else {
                    throw "PowerShell action for step $($StepDefinition.id) requires either 'scriptPath' or 'script' to be defined."
                }

                if ([string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($scriptBlockText)) {
                    throw "PowerShell script content is empty for action step $($StepDefinition.id) (from path or inline)."
                }

                $resolvedScriptText = $scriptBlockText 
                $scriptBlock = [scriptblock]::Create($resolvedScriptText)
                
                $paramsToSplat = @{}
                $paramsToSplat["ScenarioContext"] = $Context 

                if ($null -ne $externalScriptParameters) {
                    if ($externalScriptParameters -is [hashtable]) {
                        foreach ($key in $externalScriptParameters.Keys) {
                            $resolvedValue = Resolve-ContextVariables -Text $externalScriptParameters[$key] -Context $Context
                            $paramsToSplat[$key] = $resolvedValue
                            Write-Verbose "Prepared script parameter: $key = '$resolvedValue'"
                        }
                    } else {
                        Write-Warning "Step $($StepDefinition.id): action.parameters is not a hashtable. Cannot process."
                    }
                }
                
                Write-Verbose "Invoking script block via dot sourcing with splatted parameters."
                Write-Host "DEBUG: Type of scriptBlock: $($scriptBlock.GetType().FullName)" -ForegroundColor DarkCyan
                Write-Host "DEBUG: Content of scriptBlock (first 100 chars): $($scriptBlock.ToString().Substring(0, [System.Math]::Min($scriptBlock.ToString().Length, 100)) )" -ForegroundColor DarkCyan
                Write-Host "DEBUG: ParamsToSplat:" -ForegroundColor DarkCyan
                $paramsToSplat | Out-String | Write-Host -ForegroundColor DarkCyan

                try {
                    $actionOutput = . $scriptBlock @paramsToSplat 
                } catch {
                    Write-Error "ERROR during execution of external script via dot source: $($_.Exception.Message)" -ErrorAction Stop
                    throw 
                }

                $result.output = $actionOutput
                $result.success = $true
            }
            "apiCall" { 
                 throw "Action type 'apiCall' not implemented yet."
            }
             "writeFile" { 
                 throw "Action type 'writeFile' not implemented yet."
            }
            default {
                throw "Unsupported action type: $actionType"
            }
        }

        if ($result.success -and $StepDefinition.action.PSObject.Properties.Name -contains 'captureOutputVariable' -and -not [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($StepDefinition.action.captureOutputVariable) ) {
            $captureVar = $StepDefinition.action.captureOutputVariable
            Write-Verbose "Capturing action output to context variable: $captureVar"
            $Context[$captureVar] = $result.output
        }

    } catch {
        $result.error = "Error executing action $($StepDefinition.id): $($_.Exception.Message)"
        $result.success = $false
    }

    return $result
}
```

The JSON for `"parameters"` *looks* like a valid JSON object, which `ConvertFrom-Json` typically converts to a `PSCustomObject`. PowerShell often treats `PSCustomObject` like a hashtable for key access, but the `-is [hashtable]` check would be false.

The fix is to ensure that `$externalScriptParameters` is explicitly converted to a hashtable if it's a `PSCustomObject` before trying to iterate its keys.

I'll use the `Convert-PSCustomObjectToHashtable` helper function that's already in `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1`. I need to make it available in the `_step_execution.ps1` partial's scope, or redefine it there. For simplicity and to avoid scope issues with partials, I'll redefine it within `_step_execution.ps1`.

Then, I'll modify the parameter processing logic:
```powershell
                if ($null -ne $externalScriptParameters) {
                    $parametersHashtable = $null
                    if ($externalScriptParameters -is [hashtable]) {
                        $parametersHashtable = $externalScriptParameters
                    } elseif ($externalScriptParameters -is [System.Management.Automation.PSCustomObject]) {
                        $parametersHashtable = Convert-PSCustomObjectToHashtableLocal $externalScriptParameters # Using a local version
                    }

                    if ($null -ne $parametersHashtable) {
                        foreach ($key in $parametersHashtable.Keys) {
                            # ... rest of the logic ...
                        }
                    } else {
                        Write-Warning "Step $($StepDefinition.id): action.parameters is not a recognized type (hashtable or PSCustomObject). Cannot process."
                    }
                }
```

This should correctly process the parameters from the scenario JSON.

Let's start by reading the contents of `script/engine/partials/_progress_marker.ps1` (assuming this is where `Save-ProgressMarker` lives).

Read file: script/engine/partials/_progress_marker.ps1
Okay, the `Save-ProgressMarker` function in `_progress_marker.ps1` is defined as:

```powershell
function Save-ProgressMarker {
    param(
        [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][object]$ProgressMarkerData,
        [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$MarkerFilePath # <--- Correct parameter name
    )
    # ...
}
```

And `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1` calls it like this:
`Save-ProgressMarker -FilePath $progressMarkerFilePath -Status "InProgress" ...` (and other parameters which are not defined in `Save-ProgressMarker`).

The issue is a mismatch in how `Save-ProgressMarker` is *defined* versus how it's *called*.

**Definition:**
`Save-ProgressMarker` expects *two* parameters:
1.  `$ProgressMarkerData` (an object/hashtable containing all data to save)
2.  `$MarkerFilePath` (the path to save to)

**Call in `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1`:**
It's being called with named parameters that are supposed to be *inside* the `$ProgressMarkerData` object:
`-FilePath $progressMarkerFilePath` (this should be `$MarkerFilePath`)
`-Status "InProgress"` (this should be part of `$ProgressMarkerData`)
`-CurrentStepId $currentStepId` (this should be part of `$ProgressMarkerData`)
`-Context $currentContext` (this should be part of `$ProgressMarkerData`)
`-ExecutionStats $executionStats` (this should be part of `$ProgressMarkerData`)
`-ScenarioId $ScenarioId` (this should be part of `$ProgressMarkerData`)
`-ContextIdentifier $contextId` (this should be part of `$ProgressMarkerDara`)

The `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1` script needs to construct a hashtable/object containing `Status`, `CurrentStepId`, `Context`, etc., and pass *that* as the `$ProgressMarkerData` argument, and use `$MarkerFilePath` for the path.

**Example of how `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1` should call it:**
```powershell
$dataToSave = @{
    status = "InProgress" # Or $status variable
    currentStepId = $currentStepId
    context = $currentContext
    executionStats = $executionStats
    scenarioId = $ScenarioId
    contextIdentifier = $contextId
    lastUpdated = (Get-Date -Format u)
}
Save-ProgressMarker -ProgressMarkerData $dataToSave -MarkerFilePath $progressMarkerFilePath
```

I will modify `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1` to correctly prepare the `$ProgressMarkerData` object and call `Save-ProgressMarker` with the correct two parameters.

```powershell
    if ($signalContent -and $signalContent.command -eq 'stop') {
        # ...
    }
```

**`_signal_manager.ps1` content:**
```powershell
# Helper functions for managing scenario signal files

function Get-SignalPath {
    param(
        [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$ScenarioId,
        [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$SignalFileBaseDir,
        [string]$ContextIdentifier = "default"
    )
    $signalFileName = "{0}_{1}.signal.json" -f $ScenarioId, $ContextIdentifier
    return Join-Path $SignalFileBaseDir $signalFileName
}

function Get-Signal {
    param(
        [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$ScenarioId,
        [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$SignalFileBaseDir, # Changed from SignalFileBasePath
        [string]$ContextIdentifier = "default"
    )
    $signalFilePath = Get-SignalPath -ScenarioId $ScenarioId -SignalFileBaseDir $SignalFileBaseDir -ContextIdentifier $ContextIdentifier
    
    if (-not (Test-Path $signalFilePath -PathType Leaf)) {
        Write-Verbose "Signal file not found: $signalFilePath"
        return $null
    }
    try {
        $content = Get-Content -Path $signalFilePath -Raw -ErrorAction Stop
        if ([string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($content)) {
            Write-Warning "Signal file is empty: $signalFilePath"
            return $null
        }
        $signalData = $content | ConvertFrom-Json -ErrorAction Stop
        Write-Verbose "Signal data loaded from: $signalFilePath"
        return $signalData
    } catch {
        Write-Warning "Failed to load or parse signal file '$signalFilePath': $($_.Exception.Message)"
        return $null
    }
}

function Clear-Signal {
    param(
        [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$ScenarioId,
        [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$SignalFileBaseDir, # Changed from SignalFileBasePath
        [string]$ContextIdentifier = "default"
    )
    $signalFilePath = Get-SignalPath -ScenarioId $ScenarioId -SignalFileBaseDir $SignalFileBaseDir -ContextIdentifier $ContextIdentifier

    if (Test-Path $signalFilePath -PathType Leaf) {
        try {
            Remove-Item -Path $signalFilePath -Force -ErrorAction Stop
            Write-Verbose "Signal file removed: $signalFilePath"
            return $true
        } catch {
            Write-Error "Failed to remove signal file '$signalFilePath': $($_.Exception.Message)"
            return $false
        }
    } else {
        Write-Verbose "Signal file not found, nothing to clear: $signalFilePath"
        return $true # Considered success if not found
    }
}

# Example of how to create a signal (not used by engine directly, but for external trigger)
# function Set-Signal {
#     param(
#         [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$ScenarioId,
#         [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$SignalFileBaseDir,
#         [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$Command, # e.g., "stop", "pause"
#         [string]$ContextIdentifier = "default",
#         [hashtable]$Payload = @{}
#     )
#     $signalFilePath = Get-SignalPath -ScenarioId $ScenarioId -SignalFileBaseDir $SignalFileBaseDir -ContextIdentifier $ContextIdentifier
#     $signalDir = Split-Path $signalFilePath -Parent
#     if (-not (Test-Path $signalDir)) {
#         New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path $signalDir -Force | Out-Null
#     }
#     $signalData = @{
#         command = $Command
#         timestamp = (Get-Date -Format u)
#         payload = $Payload
#     }
#     $signalData | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 5 | Out-File -FilePath $signalFilePath -Encoding UTF8 -Force
#     Write-Verbose "Signal file created/updated: $signalFilePath with command $Command"
# }
```


**Path Forward:**

**`Process-NextStepIdDecision` Logic (Conceptual):**
```powershell
function Process-NextStepIdDecision {
    param(
        [PSCustomObject]$Decision, # This is $currentStepDefinition.nextStepIdDecision
        [hashtable]$Context,
        [object]$StepOutput # $stepExecutionResult.output
    )
    $nextStepId = $null
    $conditionMatched = $false

    if ($Decision.conditions) {
        foreach ($condition in $Decision.conditions) {
            # Resolve $condition.field (e.g., "context.needsUserInput") against $Context
            # Resolve $condition.value
            # Perform $condition.operator (e.g., "equals")
            # If true, $nextStepId = $condition.nextStepId; $conditionMatched = $true; break
            
            # Simplified example for "context.needsUserInput"
            if ($condition.field -eq "context.needsUserInput" -and $Context.ContainsKey("needsUserInput")) {
                $actualValue = $Context.needsUserInput
                $expectedValue = $condition.value # This might need type coercion (e.g., string to bool)
                
                # Coerce $condition.value to boolean if $actualValue is boolean
                if ($actualValue -is [bool] -and $expectedValue -is [string]) {
                    try { $expectedValue = [System.Convert]::ToBoolean($expectedValue) } catch {}
                }

                if ($condition.operator -eq "equals" -and ($actualValue -eq $expectedValue)) {
                    $nextStepId = $condition.nextStepId
                    $conditionMatched = $true
                    break
                }
            }
        }
    }

    if (-not $conditionMatched) {
        $nextStepId = $Decision.defaultNextStepId
    }
    return $nextStepId
}
```
This is a rough outline. The condition evaluation needs to be robust.

Given that `Evaluate-Transitions` in `_step_execution.ps1` already has more sophisticated condition evaluation (simple context vars, comparisons), it might be better to adapt *that* logic or refactor it into a shared helper.

For now, to get unblocked, I will add a basic `Process-NextStepIdDecision` function to `_transition_logic.ps1`. It will initially only support the exact case used by `WAIT_FOR_TASK_TYPE_ANSWER` (checking `context.needsUserInput -eq false`). We can make it more robust later.

**New `Process-NextStepIdDecision` for `_transition_logic.ps1`:**
```powershell
function Process-NextStepIdDecision {
    param(
        [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][PSCustomObject]$DecisionObject, # The 'nextStepIdDecision' object from the scenario
        [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][hashtable]$Context,
        [Parameter(Mandatory=$false)]$StepOutput # Output from the previous step (passed as stepExecutionResult.output)
    )

    $nextStepId = $null
    $conditionMatched = $false

    Write-Verbose "Processing nextStepIdDecision..."

    if ($null -ne $DecisionObject.conditions) {
        foreach ($condition in $DecisionObject.conditions) {
            $fieldPath = $condition.field
            $operator = $condition.operator
            $expectedValueLiteral = $condition.value # This is the literal value from the JSON

            Write-Verbose "Evaluating condition: Field:'$fieldPath' $operator '$expectedValueLiteral'"

            if ($fieldPath -eq "context.needsUserInput") {
                if ($Context.ContainsKey("needsUserInput")) {
                    $actualValue = $Context["needsUserInput"]
                    
                    # Convert expectedValueLiteral (string from JSON) to boolean for comparison
                    $expectedValueBool = $false
                    if ($expectedValueLiteral -is [string] -and $expectedValueLiteral.ToLower() -eq "false") {
                        $expectedValueBool = $false
                    } elseif ($expectedValueLiteral -is [string] -and $expectedValueLiteral.ToLower() -eq "true") {
                        $expectedValueBool = $true
                    } elseif ($expectedValueLiteral -is [bool]) {
                        $expectedValueBool = $expectedValueLiteral
                    } else {
                        Write-Warning "Cannot reliably convert expected value '$expectedValueLiteral' to boolean for 'context.needsUserInput' comparison."
                        continue # Skip this condition
                    }

                    if ($operator -eq "equals") {
                        if ($actualValue -is [bool] -and $actualValue -eq $expectedValueBool) {
                            $nextStepId = $condition.nextStepId
                            $conditionMatched = $true
                            Write-Verbose "Condition matched: $fieldPath ($actualValue) $operator $expectedValueBool. Next step: $nextStepId"
                            break
                        } else {
                             Write-Verbose "Condition NOT matched: $fieldPath ($actualValue) $operator $expectedValueBool."
                        }
                    } else {
                        Write-Warning "Unsupported operator '$operator' for field '$fieldPath' in nextStepIdDecision."
                    }
                } else {
                    Write-Warning "Context field '$fieldPath' not found for condition in nextStepIdDecision."
                }
            } else {
                Write-Warning "Unsupported field '$fieldPath' in nextStepIdDecision. Only 'context.needsUserInput' is currently handled by this basic implementation."
            }
        }
    }

    if (-not $conditionMatched) {
        $nextStepId = $DecisionObject.defaultNextStepId
        Write-Verbose "No specific condition matched or no conditions defined. Using defaultNextStepId: $nextStepId"
    }
    
    Write-Verbose "Process-NextStepIdDecision determined next step: $nextStepId"
    return $nextStepId
}
```
This function is more specific to the current need. I'll add this to `_transition_logic.ps1`.


The `Process-NextStepIdDecision` function has been added to `script/engine/partials/_transition_logic.ps1`.
This function should now be sourced correctly by `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1`, and the "term not recognized" error for it should be resolved.

The current state of the Q&A flow:
1.  `GENERATE_TASK_TYPE_QUESTION` (Action step using `GenerateQuestion.ps1`) ran.
    *   `GenerateQuestion.ps1` created a question (e.g., `QID-3bd0d9b2`) and put its ID into the context as `pendingQuestionId`.
2.  `WAIT_FOR_TASK_TYPE_ANSWER` (Action step using `GetAnswerAndUpdateContext.ps1`) ran.
    *   `GetAnswerAndUpdateContext.ps1` didn't find an answer for `QID-3bd0d9b2` in `answers.json`.
    *   It set `context.needsUserInput = $true` and `context.statusMessage`.
    *   The scenario engine detected `needsUserInput = $true` and should have saved progress and exited (which it did, but then failed trying to call `Process-NextStepIdDecision`).

Now, when `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1` calls `Process-NextStepIdDecision` for the `WAIT_FOR_TASK_TYPE_ANSWER` step:
*   `$Context.needsUserInput` will be `$true`.
*   The condition in `nextStepIdDecision` is `context.needsUserInput equals false`.
*   This condition will be `$false`.
*   So, `Process-NextStepIdDecision` should return the `defaultNextStepId`, which is `WAIT_FOR_TASK_TYPE_ANSWER`.
*   The engine will loop back to `WAIT_FOR_TASK_TYPE_ANSWER`.
*   Since `$stepExecutionResult.needsUserInput` from the *previous* execution of `WAIT_FOR_TASK_TYPE_ANSWER` was `$true`, the `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1` should then hit this block:
    ```powershell
    if ($stepExecutionResult.needsUserInput) {
        $status = "AwaitingInput" 
        # ... save progress ...
        Write-Host ("Scenario '$ScenarioId' is now $status for step '$currentStepId'. Question: $($stepExecutionResult.questionTextToDisplay)")
        $finalResult = @{ status = $status; context = $currentContext; nextStepId = $currentStepId; question = $stepExecutionResult.questionTextToDisplay }
        return $finalResult # Exit script, AI/user needs to provide input
    }
    ```
This means the engine should now correctly halt with status `AwaitingInput`.

Okay, this output is very informative!

1.  **`Process-NextStepIdDecision` is working:** The "term not recognized" error for it is gone.
2.  **Looping correctly:**
    *   The `Process-NextStepIdDecision` correctly evaluated that `context.needsUserInput` is `$true`.
    *   Therefore, it returned the `defaultNextStepId`, which is `WAIT_FOR_TASK_TYPE_ANSWER`.
    *   The engine then looped back to the `WAIT_FOR_TASK_TYPE_ANSWER` step.
3.  **Infinite Loop Prevented:** The engine repeatedly executed `WAIT_FOR_TASK_TYPE_ANSWER` because `needsUserInput` remained true (as no answer was provided externally). This continued until the `MaxSteps` (default 100) was reached, and the engine correctly halted with the error:
    `Maximum step count (100) reached. Halting scenario to prevent infinite loop.`

This is *almost* the desired behavior for a step awaiting input. The missing piece is that `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1` should have exited *gracefully* (not with an error) when `stepExecutionResult.needsUserInput` was true, *before* looping back and hitting the max step count.

The logic in `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1` for handling `needsUserInput` is:
```powershell
    # ... (after Execute-Step and context merge) ...

    if ($stepExecutionResult.needsUserInput) {
        $status = "AwaitingInput" 
        $progressDataAwaitingInput = @{
            status            = $status
            currentStepId     = $currentStepId
            context           = $currentContext
            executionStats    = $executionStats
            scenarioId        = $ScenarioId
            contextIdentifier = $contextId
            question          = $stepExecutionResult.questionTextToDisplay # Include the question
            lastUpdated       = (Get-Date -Format u)
        }
        Save-ProgressMarker -ProgressMarkerData $progressDataAwaitingInput -MarkerFilePath $progressMarkerFilePath
        Write-Host ("Scenario '$ScenarioId' is now $status for step '$currentStepId'. Question: $($stepExecutionResult.questionTextToDisplay)") -ForegroundColor Yellow
        Write-Host "To provide input, re-run with -UserInputValue 'YourAnswer' or interactively if allowed." -ForegroundColor Yellow
        
        # Prepare the final result object for the engine to return
        $finalResult = @{ 
            status = $status; 
            context = $currentContext; # Return the latest context
            currentStepId = $currentStepId; # The step that is awaiting input
            question = $stepExecutionResult.questionTextToDisplay 
        }
        return $finalResult # EXIT SCRIPT
    }

    # Determine next step ID (this is where Process-NextStepIdDecision is called)
    # ...
```

The issue is that the `return $finalResult` (which should exit `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1`) is *inside* the `if ($stepExecutionResult.needsUserInput)` block. However, the transition logic (calling `Process-NextStepIdDecision`) happens *after* this block.

If `needsUserInput` is true, the engine *should*:
1.  Save the "AwaitingInput" status.
2.  Print the messages.
3.  Return `$finalResult` and **exit immediately**.

It seems it *is* saving and printing, but then it proceeds to the transition logic, which causes the loop.

The problem might be how the script is structured or if there's another path where the loop continues despite `needsUserInput` being true.

Let's look at the `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1` around line 470-500 (where the main loop and `needsUserInput` check are).

Read file: script/engine/invoke-scenario-engine.php
The code structure is correct. The `return $finalResult` statement is indeed present inside the `if ($stepExecutionResult.needsUserInput)` block.
This `return` should terminate the `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1` script itself, not just exit the current function or loop block.

The log output clearly shows that the lines *inside* this `if` block are being executed:
```
[DEBUG Invoke-Engine] === AFTER Execute-Step & Merge: WAIT_FOR_TASK_TYPE_ANSWER ===
[DEBUG Invoke-Engine] Context: {"pendingQuestionId":"QID-3bd0d9b2","statusMessage":"Waiting for a 'new' answer to question ID: QID-3bd0d9b2","needsUserInput":true}
True <--- This "True" is an anomaly, probably a leftover Write-Host.
```
(The `True` line seems to be an errant `Write-Host $stepExecutionResult.needsUserInput` or similar that I might have added during debugging and not removed from the file in `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1`).

Then the loop continues, indicating `return $finalResult` did not terminate the script as expected.

In PowerShell, `return` from within a script (not a function) should exit the script. If `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1` is being dot-sourced by another script (like `main-work.ps1`), then `return` would exit `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1` and return control to the caller. If it's run directly (`pwsh -File Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1`), it should terminate the process.

The only reason it might not be exiting is if `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1` is itself wrapped in a function *within that same file* and the main script body calls that wrapper function. But looking at the structure, the `while` loop and the `if ($stepExecutionResult.needsUserInput)` block appear to be at the top level of the script body, after the `param()` block and function definitions.

Let's find and remove that stray `True` output first, as it's cluttering the log and might indicate a point of confusion. It's likely just before or after the `if ($stepExecutionResult.needsUserInput)` block.

Then, if the script still doesn't exit, we might need to force an exit more explicitly when `needsUserInput` is true, e.g., using `exit 0` (for success, indicating it's waiting for input) after setting `$finalResult`. However, `return $finalResult` *should* be sufficient and is cleaner as it passes back data.

I'll look for that stray `Write-Host` outputting boolean `True` around the `needsUserInput` check in `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1`.


No stray `Write-Host` was found by the model that could be causing that `True` output. This is puzzling.

The `True` appears in the log right after:
`[DEBUG Invoke-Engine] Context: {"pendingQuestionId":"QID-3bd0d9b2","statusMessage":"Waiting for a 'new' answer to question ID: QID-3bd0d9b2","needsUserInput":true}`

And before the next step's `--- Step XX: WAIT_FOR_TASK_TYPE_ANSWER ---` header.

This position is *after* the context merge and *before* the error handling or `needsUserInput` block.

Let's look at `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1` specifically from around line 440 to 480 to see if there's *any* direct `Write-Host` that's not part of a conditional debug message, or if a variable is being output directly.

Read file: script/engine/invoke-scenario-engine.php
I've reviewed lines 440-480 of `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1` and the surrounding code. There's no obvious stray `Write-Host` that would unconditionally print `True`.

The block of code is:
```powershell
    # ... (after Execute-Step)
    Write-Host "[DEBUG Invoke-Engine] === AFTER Execute-Step & Merge: $currentStepId ====" -ForegroundColor Yellow
    Write-Host "[DEBUG Invoke-Engine] Context: $($currentContext | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 5 -Compress)" -ForegroundColor Gray

    # Error Handling for step execution
    if (-not $stepExecutionResult.success) {
        # ... error handling logic ...
    }

    # Check for Halt condition from step (e.g. Question step needs input)
    if ($stepExecutionResult.needsUserInput) {
        # ... logic to set status to AwaitingInput, save progress, print messages ...
        return $finalResult # Exit script
    }

    # Transition to next step
    # ...
```

The `True` output in the log appears immediately *after* the `Context:` debug line.
My best guess is that some object or variable is unintentionally being sent to the output stream pipeline *before* the `Error Handling` or `needsUserInput` blocks are checked.

If `Execute-Step` or one of the functions it calls (like `Execute-StepAction`, or the action scripts themselves e.g. `GetAnswerAndUpdateContext.ps1`) outputs a boolean `$true` to the pipeline *without* `Write-Host` or `Write-Verbose`, it could appear in the standard output.

`GetAnswerAndUpdateContext.ps1` does return a hashtable. If, for some reason, it also accidentally output `$true` directly, that might explain it.

For now, this stray `True` is a minor formatting issue and doesn't seem to be the cause of the looping behavior. The primary issue was that `return $finalResult` was not exiting the script when `needsUserInput` was true, leading to the max steps error.

Since the `return $finalResult` statement *is* correctly placed, and it *should* terminate a script when executed at the script's top level (not inside a function defined and called within the same script), its failure to do so is perplexing if `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1` is run directly.

If `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1` is itself dot-sourced by `main-work.ps1` (or another script), then `return $finalResult` would correctly return the hashtable to `main-work.ps1`, and `main-work.ps1` would then be responsible for stopping further scenario execution if the status is `AwaitingInput`.

Let's assume for a moment that `main-work.ps1` *is* the caller and is not yet handling the `AwaitingInput` status correctly from the returned `$finalResult`.
The immediate fix within `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1` to ensure it stops when run standalone and `needsUserInput` is true, is to use `exit 0` after preparing `$finalResult`. This is less clean for library-style usage but will guarantee termination.

However, before doing that, there's one more thing to check in `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1`. The script has a `param()` block at the top. If the main execution logic (the `while` loop) is accidentally wrapped inside a function definition *later* in the script, and the script only calls this wrapper function, then `return` would only exit that wrapper function, not the script.

I need to see the very beginning and very end of `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1` to ensure the main `while` loop is not accidentally inside an unintentionally defined function.

Read file: script/engine/invoke-scenario-engine.php
The beginning of `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1` shows:
1.  `CmdletBinding()` and `param(...)` block.
2.  A helper function `Convert-PSCustomObjectToHashtable` defined directly in the script.
3.  Sourcing of partials.
4.  Engine configuration setup.
5.  Initialization and parameter validation, including an `exit 0` if no `ScenarioId` is provided.
6.  Then the script proceeds to load the scenario, progress marker, and then enters the main `while` loop.

The `while` loop is indeed at the top level of the script body, not inside any other function defined in this file.
Therefore, `return $finalResult` from within the `if ($stepExecutionResult.needsUserInput)` block *should* terminate the `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1` script and return the `$finalResult` hashtable.

Why it was looping until `MaxSteps` is still a bit of a mystery if `needsUserInput` was true.
Perhaps the `return $finalResult` line was not reached due to an error *before* it within that `if` block, or the condition was somehow false in later iterations?

The logs showed the messages "Scenario ... is now AwaitingInput..." and "To provide input, re-run..." which are *inside* the `if ($stepExecutionResult.needsUserInput)` block and *before* the `return $finalResult`. So, the block *was* being entered.

Could the issue be the definition of `$finalResult` *inside* that block versus the `$finalResult` defined at the very end of the script?
PowerShell variable scope can be tricky.

Inside the `if ($stepExecutionResult.needsUserInput)` block:
`$finalResult = @{ status = $status; context = $currentContext; nextStepId = $currentStepId; question = $stepExecutionResult.questionTextToDisplay }`
`return $finalResult`

At the very end of the script (after the loop):
`$finalResult = @{ status = $status; context = $currentContext; currentStepId = $currentStepId; error = $errorMessage; executionStats = $executionStats }`
`return $finalResult`

These are distinct assignments. The `return` inside the `if` block should take precedence and exit.

The only way the loop continued is if:
1.  The `return $finalResult` statement inside the `if ($stepExecutionResult.needsUserInput)` block was somehow skipped (e.g., an error occurred immediately before it, like in `Save-ProgressMarker` or `Write-Host`). But we fixed `Save-ProgressMarker` and saw the `Write-Host` messages print.
2.  Or, in subsequent iterations of the loop, `$stepExecutionResult.needsUserInput` became `$false` (e.g., if `$currentUserInputValue` was somehow populated non-null for the next iteration, even though it's set to `$null` after each step). This seems unlikely.

Let's focus on the most direct cause: why didn't `return $finalResult` stop the script execution and prevent the loop when `needsUserInput` was true?

If the script is run as `pwsh -File Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1`, then `return` should indeed exit the script with the returned value as the exit code (if it's an integer) or the object can be captured by a caller. If it's not exiting, it's almost as if the PowerShell session itself is forcing it to continue or the `return` isn't behaving as a script-level termination.

For absolute certainty of termination when `needsUserInput` is true, I will add an `exit 0` immediately after the `return $finalResult` statement. While `return` should be enough, `exit` is more forceful for script termination. This is a diagnostic step. If `exit 0` works, it points to `return` behaving unexpectedly in this execution context.

I'll add `exit 0 # Force exit when AwaitingInput` right after `return $finalResult`.

Read file: script/engine/invoke-scenario-engine.php
The logic for `-ResetProgress` (which now corresponds to the `$ScenarioCommand "reset"`) is:
```powershell
        "reset" {
            Write-Host "Resetting scenario state. Removing progress marker..."
            Remove-ProgressMarker -MarkerFilePath $markerFilePath # $markerFilePath is already determined
            $progressMarker = $null
            $currentStepId = $null # Will start from scenario.startStepId
            $context = $InitialContext.Clone() # Reset context
            $stepCounter = 0 # This was $executionStats.stepCounter, should be $executionStats itself
            $ScenarioCommand = $null 
            $status = "Halted" # Halts further execution in this run
            Write-Host "Scenario progress reset. Halting further execution in this run."
        }
```
Then, later, progress loading happens:
```powershell
if ((Test-Path $progressMarkerFilePath) -and (-not $ResetProgress)) { 
    # ... loads progress marker ...
}
```

The problem is the `$ResetProgress` parameter is a *script parameter*, but the `ScenarioCommand "reset"` logic correctly sets internal variables like `$progressMarker = $null` and `$currentStepId = $null`. However, the loading block for an existing marker still checks `-not $ResetProgress` (the script param).

If `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1 -ResetProgress $true` is called:
1.  The `$ResetProgress` script parameter is `$true`.
2.  The `$ScenarioCommand` is likely `$null` (not "reset").
3.  The `if ((Test-Path $markerFilePath) -and (-not $ResetProgress))` block for loading an *existing* marker will be SKIPPED because `-not $ResetProgress` is `-not $true` which is `$false`.
4.  Then, `if (($null -eq $progressMarker) -or $ResetProgress)` will be TRUE (because `$ResetProgress` is true).
5.  This correctly initializes new progress: `$currentStepId = $scenarioDefinition.startStepId`, `$executionStats` is reset.

This flow for `-ResetProgress $true` looks correct.

Ah, a simple but critical mistake in my command!
The error is: `Cannot process argument transformation on parameter 'ResetProgress'. Cannot convert value "System.String" to type "System.Boolean".`

I've been using `-ResetProgress $true` in my `run_terminal_cmd`.
In PowerShell, when calling a script with a switch or boolean parameter, you provide the value like this: `-ResetProgress:$true` or `-ResetProgress:$false`.
If you just use `-ResetProgress $true`, the `$true` is interpreted as a separate positional argument, and `-ResetProgress` (if it's a `[switch]` or a `[bool]` expecting a following value) might misinterpret or the parser fails as seen.

The parameter is defined as: `[bool]$ResetProgress = $false`.
So, the correct way to pass it is `-ResetProgress:$true`.

My apologies for that oversight. Let's try the command again with the correct syntax.

3.  Print my debug message: `DEBUG: Initializing new progress...`
4.  Initialize `$currentStepId = $scenarioDefinition.startStepId` and reset `$executionStats`.
5.  Print my second debug message: `DEBUG: NEW progress initialized...`
6.  Save this new initial progress marker.
7.  Then proceed to the main `while` loop *with the reset state*.

The output provided for the `-ResetProgress:$true` command does *not* show these magenta debug lines:
`DEBUG: Initializing new progress. ResetProgress flag: True. ProgressMarker is null: True`
`DEBUG: NEW progress initialized. CurrentStepId: GENERATE_TASK_TYPE_QUESTION, Status: InProgress, StepCounter: 0`

Instead, it immediately goes into the loop (`--- Step 85: WAIT_FOR_TASK_TYPE_ANSWER ---`), implying it loaded an *old* progress marker.

This means the logic for handling `-ResetProgress:$true` is flawed. The script parameter `$ResetProgress` is not being correctly used to prevent loading of an old marker or to force the initialization path.

Let's re-examine the section in `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1`:
```powershell
# ... (script params including [bool]$ResetProgress = $false) ...

# --- Progress Marker & Context Initialization ---
# ...
$executionStats = @{ stepCounter = 0; startTime = (Get-Date -Format u); retriesRemaining = @{} }

# This is the block that LOADS existing progress if -ResetProgress is FALSE
if ((Test-Path $progressMarkerFilePath) -and (-not $ResetProgress)) { 
    Write-Verbose "Loading existing progress marker: $progressMarkerFilePath"
    # ... loads $progressMarker, $currentContext, $currentStepId, $executionStats, $status ...
}

# This is the block that INITIALIZES NEW progress if marker was not loaded OR if -ResetProgress is TRUE
if (($null -eq $progressMarker) -or $ResetProgress) { 
    # My debug lines are here
    Write-Host "DEBUG: Initializing new progress. ResetProgress flag: $ResetProgress. ProgressMarker is null: $($null -eq $progressMarker)" -ForegroundColor Magenta
    # ... sets $currentStepId = $scenarioDefinition.startStepId ...
    # ... resets $executionStats ...
    # ... sets $status = "InProgress" ...
    Write-Host "DEBUG: NEW progress initialized. CurrentStepId: $currentStepId, Status: $status, StepCounter: $($executionStats.stepCounter)" -ForegroundColor Magenta
    # ... saves new marker ...
}
```
If `-ResetProgress:$true` is passed:
1.  `(-not $ResetProgress)` becomes `(-not $true)` which is `$false`.
2.  So the first `if` condition `(Test-Path ...) -and $false` is `$false`. The existing progress marker is NOT loaded. `$progressMarker` remains `$null`.
3.  The second `if` condition `($null -eq $progressMarker) -or $ResetProgress` becomes `($true) -or $true`, which is `$true`.
4.  This *should* enter the "Initializing new progress" block, print my magenta debug lines, and reset all state variables.

The output from the run with `-ResetProgress:$true` did *not* show my magenta debug lines, and instead showed it starting from `Step 85`. This is a direct contradiction of the logic flow I just outlined.

The only way this can happen is if the `$ResetProgress` variable, despite being passed as `$true` on the command line, is not actually `$true` when these `if` conditions are evaluated.

Could there be a scope issue where the script parameter `$ResetProgress` is shadowed by another variable named `$ResetProgress` locally before these checks? (Unlikely, but let's check).

Or, is there an earlier `Remove-ProgressMarker` call that happens *unconditionally* if `-ResetProgress` is true, but then the logic *still* tries to load it and fails, then somehow skips re-initialization?

The `ScenarioCommand "reset"` logic is separate:
```powershell
        "reset" { # This is for -ScenarioCommand "reset"
            Write-Host "Resetting scenario state. Removing progress marker..."
            Remove-ProgressMarker -MarkerFilePath $markerFilePath
            # ... sets $currentStepId = $null, $progressMarker = $null etc.
            $status = "Halted" 
        }
```
If `-ResetProgress:$true` is used (the script parameter), the `ScenarioCommand` is usually not "reset".
The script parameter `$ResetProgress` should directly control the two `if` blocks for loading/initializing progress.

4.  A mystery `True` gets printed.
5.  The loop continues.

This means `$stepExecutionResult.needsUserInput` changes from `True` (as returned by `Execute-Step` and seen in its debug output) to `False` *before* the `if` condition is evaluated, or *as part of the `if` condition evaluation in a way that changes it*.

This is deeply strange. The `$stepExecutionResult` is a hashtable.
Could the issue be in `Execute-Step` itself, specifically in `Execute-StepQuestion`?

Let's look at `Execute-StepQuestion` in `_step_execution.ps1`:
```powershell
function Execute-StepQuestion {
    param(
        # ... params ...
        [Parameter(Mandatory=$false)][string]$UserInputValue,
        # ...
    )
    $result = @{ 
        # ...
        needsUserInput = $false; # Initialized to false
        # ...
    }
    # ...
    if ($null -ne $UserInputValue) {
        # ...
        $result.inputValueConsumed = $true
    } else {
        # ... (interactive check)
        if (-not $isInteractive) {
            # ... error ...
            return $result # success is false, needsUserInput is still false
        }
        $result.needsUserInput = $true # Set to true here
        $result.success = $true 
        Write-Verbose "Step $($StepDefinition.id) requires interactive input. Signaling engine."
        return $result # Return immediately, needsUserInput is true
    }

    # --- Validation ---
    if ($StepDefinition.input.validation -and $StepDefinition.input.validation.required -and [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($answer)) {
        # ... error ...
        # needsUserInput remains as it was (either from above, or still initial false if UserInputValue was given but empty)
    } else {
         $result.output = $answer 
         $result.success = $true
         # needsUserInput remains as it was
    }
    return $result
}
```
If `$UserInputValue` is `$null` (which it is when `-ResetProgress:$true` is run without `-UserInputValue`), and interactive input is allowed (default), then `$result.needsUserInput` is set to `$true`, and `$result` is returned immediately. This part seems correct.

The value of `$stepExecutionResult.needsUserInput` *must* be flipping to `$false` between its return from `Execute-StepQuestion` (and thus `Execute-Step`) and the `if` statement in `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1`.

What if the mystery `True` is the actual boolean value `$true` being output by one of the `Write-Host` lines that use `-ForegroundColor` when `$Green` or `$Yellow` or `$Gray` are not defined because `_Initialize-Colors.ps1` is not sourced?
The summary mentioned:
> Cause 2: Write-Host "[DEBUG] Engine start" -ForegroundColor Green in Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1. Since _Initialize-Colors.ps1 (which defined $Green) was no longer sourced, $Green was $null, causing an error with -ForegroundColor.
> Fix 2: Removed -ForegroundColor Green.

I've added many `Write-Host ... -ForegroundColor ...` lines. If any of those color variables are undefined, PowerShell might try to output the string `-ForegroundColor` and then the `$null` variable, and then if the next part of the `Write-Host` is a boolean true, that might be getting printed raw.

Let's remove ALL `-ForegroundColor` options from all `Write-Host` statements in `Invoke-ScenarioEngine.ps1` and `_step_execution.ps1` as a diagnostic step. This is a bit broad, but the "Max steps reached" implies a loop control problem, and the mystery `True` + the `needsUserInput` flip is the prime suspect for breaking that control.

```powershell
# Content of _step_execution.ps1 (first 200 lines)
# Functions related to the execution of individual scenario steps and transitions

# Helper function (can be defined at the top of the partial or ensured available)
function Convert-PSCustomObjectToHashtableLocal { # Renamed to avoid conflict if sourced globally
    param($InputObject)
    if ($null -eq $InputObject) { return $null }
    $hashtable = @{}
    if ($InputObject -is [System.Management.Automation.PSCustomObject]) {
        $InputObject.PSObject.Properties | ForEach-Object {
            $hashtable[$_.Name] = Convert-PSCustomObjectToHashtableLocal $_.Value # Recursive call for nested objects
        }
    } elseif ($InputObject -is [array]) {
        # If it's an array, iterate and convert each element
        $array = @()
        foreach ($item in $InputObject) {
            $array += Convert-PSCustomObjectToHashtableLocal $item
        }
        return $array
    } elseif ($InputObject -is [hashtable]) {
        # If it's already a hashtable, clone it to ensure no side effects and convert nested PSCustomObjects
        foreach($key in $InputObject.Keys) {
            $hashtable[$key] = Convert-PSCustomObjectToHashtableLocal $InputObject[$key]
        }
    } else {
        # For other types, return as is
        return $InputObject
    }
    return $hashtable
}

function Evaluate-Transitions {
    param(
        [array]$Transitions,
        [hashtable]$Context,
        [object]$StepResult # Contains $StepResult.inputValue if applicable
    )

    $nextStepId = $null
    $transitionMatched = $false

    if (-not $Transitions) {
        Write-Verbose "No transitions defined for this step. Scenario ends."
        return $null # End of scenario if no transitions
    }

    # Process non-default transitions first
    foreach ($transition in $Transitions) {
        if ($transition.condition -eq 'default') { continue }

        $conditionMet = $false
        $rawCondition = $transition.condition # Keep original for logging
        Write-Verbose "Evaluating condition: '$rawCondition' for next step '$($transition.nextStepId)'"

        try {
            # --- NEW EVALUATION LOGIC ---
            # 1. Check for simple boolean context variable: {{context.varName}}
            $simpleVarMatch = $rawCondition -match '^\{\{context\.([a-zA-Z0-9_]+)\}\}$'
            if ($simpleVarMatch) {
                $varName = $matches[1]
                Write-Verbose "Condition is simple context variable: $varName"
                if ($Context.ContainsKey($varName)) {
                    $contextValue = $Context[$varName]
                    if (($contextValue -is [bool] -and $contextValue -eq $true) -or 
                        ($contextValue -is [string] -and $contextValue -ieq 'True')) {
                        $conditionMet = $true
                    } else {
                        $conditionMet = $false
                        Write-Verbose "Simple boolean context variable '$varName' is not True (Value: '$contextValue')"
                    }
                } else {
                    Write-Warning "Simple context variable '$varName' not found in context. Condition is false."
                    $conditionMet = $false
                }
            } else {
                 # 2. Check for comparison: {{context.varName}} -op 'value' / value
                 $comparisonMatch = $rawCondition -match '^\{\{context\.([a-zA-Z0-9_]+)\}\}\s*(-eq|-ne|-gt|-ge|-lt|-le)\s*(.*)$'
                 if ($comparisonMatch) {
                     $varName = $matches[1]
                     $operator = $matches[2]
                     $rightOperandLiteral = $matches[3].Trim()
                     Write-Verbose "Condition is comparison: context.$varName $operator '$rightOperandLiteral'"

                     if ($Context.ContainsKey($varName)) {
                         $leftValue = $Context[$varName]
                         
                         # Clean right operand literal (remove quotes)
                         if (($rightOperandLiteral.StartsWith("'") -and $rightOperandLiteral.EndsWith("'")) -or 
                             ($rightOperandLiteral.StartsWith('"') -and $rightOperandLiteral.EndsWith('"'))) {
                             $rightOperandLiteral = $rightOperandLiteral.Substring(1, $rightOperandLiteral.Length - 2)
                         }

                         # --- DEBUG LOG ---
                         Write-Verbose "Performing comparison. Left Value from Context ('$varName'): '$($leftValue)' (Type: $(if($null -ne $leftValue){$leftValue.GetType().FullName}else{'<null>'}))"
                         # --- END DEBUG LOG ---

                         # Perform comparison (Try numeric first if applicable)
                         $leftNum = $null
                         $rightNum = $null
                         $isNumericComparison = $false
                         if (($leftValue -is [int] -or $leftValue -is [long] -or $leftValue -is [double] -or $leftValue -match '^\d+(\.\d+)?$') -and 
                             ($rightOperandLiteral -match '^\d+(\.\d+)?$' -and 
                             ($operator -match '-g|-l'))) {
                             try {
                                 # Use decimal for potentially more precision if needed, convert back for PS compare?
                                 # Or just use double
                                 $leftNum = [double]$leftValue
                                 $rightNum = [double]$rightOperandLiteral
                                 $isNumericComparison = $true
                             } catch { $isNumericComparison = $false }
                         }

                        Write-Verbose "Comparing (L: '$leftValue', R: '$rightOperandLiteral', Op: $operator, Numeric: $isNumericComparison)"
                        try {
                            if ($isNumericComparison) {
                                switch ($operator) {
                                    '-gt' { $conditionMet = ($leftNum -gt $rightNum) }
                                    '-ge' { $conditionMet = ($leftNum -ge $rightNum) }
                                    '-lt' { $conditionMet = ($leftNum -lt $rightNum) }
                                    '-le' { $conditionMet = ($leftNum -le $rightNum) }
                                    '-eq' { $conditionMet = ($leftNum -eq $rightNum) } # Numeric eq
                                    '-ne' { $conditionMet = ($leftNum -ne $rightNum) } # Numeric ne
                                    default { Write-Warning "Unsupported numeric operator '$operator'"
                                    $conditionMet = $false }
                                }
                            } else { # String comparison (case sensitive for -eq/-ne by default)
                                switch ($operator) {
                                    '-eq' { $conditionMet = ($leftValue -eq $rightOperandLiteral) } # String eq
                                    '-ne' { $conditionMet = ($leftValue -ne $rightOperandLiteral) } # String ne
                                    # Add case-insensitive options if needed using -ieq/-ine
                                    default { Write-Warning "Unsupported string operator '$operator' (only -eq, -ne allowed)"
                                    $conditionMet = $false }
                                }
                            }
                        } catch {
                            Write-Warning "Error performing comparison context.$varName $operator '$rightOperandLiteral' (Values: '$leftValue', '$rightOperandLiteral'): $($_.Exception.Message)"
                            $conditionMet = $false
                        }
                     } else {
                        Write-Warning "Context variable '$varName' for comparison not found. Condition is false."
                        $conditionMet = $false
                     }
                 } else {
                     # 3. If not simple boolean or comparison, treat as unsupported/false
                     Write-Warning "Condition '$rawCondition' is not a recognized simple boolean or comparison format. Treating as false."
                     $conditionMet = $false
                 }
            }
            # --- END NEW EVALUATION LOGIC ---
        } catch {
            Write-Warning "Error evaluating condition '$rawCondition': $($_.Exception.Message)"
            $conditionMet = $false
        }

        if ($conditionMet -eq $true) {
            $nextStepId = $transition.nextStepId
            $transitionMatched = $true
            Write-Verbose "Condition matched. Next step: $nextStepId"
            break
        }
    }

    # If no condition matched, check for a default transition
    if (-not $transitionMatched) {
        $defaultTransition = $Transitions | Where-Object { $_.condition -eq 'default' } | Select-Object -First 1
        if ($defaultTransition) {
            $nextStepId = $defaultTransition.nextStepId
            Write-Verbose "No specific condition matched. Using default transition. Next step: $nextStepId"
        } else {
            Write-Verbose "No specific condition matched and no default transition found. Scenario ends."
            $nextStepId = $null # Explicitly set to null if no match and no default
        }
    }

    return $nextStepId
}

function Execute-Step {
    param(
        [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][PSCustomObject]$StepDefinition,
        [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][hashtable]$Context,
        [Parameter(Mandatory=$false)][string]$UserInputValue = $null,
        [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][hashtable]$EngineConfiguration
    )

    # --- BEGIN DEBUG ---    
    Write-Host "DEBUG [Execute-Step]: Received EngineConfiguration keys: $($EngineConfiguration.Keys | Out-String)"
    if ($EngineConfiguration.ContainsKey('EnginePSScriptRoot')) {
        Write-Host "DEBUG [Execute-Step]: EnginePSScriptRoot IS PRESENT in Execute-Step. Value: $($EngineConfiguration.EnginePSScriptRoot)"
    } else {
        Write-Warning "DEBUG [Execute-Step]: EnginePSScriptRoot IS MISSING in Execute-Step!"
    }
    # --- END DEBUG --- 

    $stepResult = @{
        success = $false;
        output = $null;
        error = $null;
        # Flags to be populated by sub-functions like Execute-StepQuestion
        inputValueConsumed = $false; 
        needsUserInput = $false;
        questionTextToDisplay = $null
        # haltProcessing was considered but logic is now: if needsUserInput, engine halts.
    }

    try {
        $stepType = $StepDefinition.type
```
